faoe oourse, sealing and placing oover aggregate on the new East-West Runway,

                                  SECTION 130

                             AGGREGATE BASE COURSE


                                 DESCRIPTION


     131-01   Description.    This item shall provide for the oonstruotion of
a base course composed of orush~d;,aggreg~te, constructed on a prepared
underlying course in aooordance with these speoifioations, and in conform-
ity with the dimensions and typical cross sections shown on the Plans, and
with the lines and grades established by the En~ineer.

                                  'MATE..'tIALS


     132-01 Crushed Coarse A,ggregate. The aggregates shall c~nsist of
both fine and coarse fragments of crushed stone, or crushed gravel, blended
it required, with sand, screenings or other s im.ilar approved materials. The
completed mixture shall be capable of being compacted into a dense and well-
bonded base.

     The crushed stone shall consist of hard, durable particles or fragments
of stone, free from an excess of flat, elongated, soft or disintegrated
pieces, dirt or other objectionable matter and shall have a percent of water
of not more than 50 at 500 revolutions as determined by A.A.S.H.O. Method
T-96-42 (Los Angeles Rattler Test).
     The crushed gravel shall consist of hard, durable stones, rook and
boulders or aooepted quality orushed to speoified size and shall be free
from an excess of flat, elongated, soft or disintegrated pieoee, dirt or
other objectionable matter. The method used in produotion of crushed gravel
shall be such that the percentage of fractured. particles occurring in the
finished product shall be as ·nearly constant and uniform as practicable. The
crushing of the gravel shall result in a product that at least sixty (60)
percent of the material passing a l" sieve and retained on a 3/4" sieve and
that at lea~t forty-five (45) percent of the material passing a 3/4" sieve
and retained on a No. 4 sieve will have at·least one fractured faoe. If
necessary, to meet this requirement or to eliminate an excess or fine, un-
crushed particles, the gravel shall be screened before crushing. All stones.,
rocks and boulders of inferior q_uality ooourring in the pit shall be
separated-out and wasted before crushing. The gravel shall ha-ve a percent
of wear of not more than 50 at 500 revolutions as determined by A.A.S.H.O.,
Nethod T 96-42 (Los Angeles Rattler Test).

     132-02 Gradation. The gl'.'ad.s.t1on of the processed or unprocessed
material shall meet the requirements of the gradation Given in the follow•
ing table, determined by A.A.s.u.o. Methods T-11-42 and T-27-42.

     The portion of the material passing the No. 200 sieve shall be obtained
from the crushing operation without the addition of soil.

     The amount of the fraction of material passing the No. 200 sieve shall
not exoeed one-half the fraotion passing the No. 40 sieve.
     The gradation in this table represents the limits which shall determine
suitability of aggregate for use from the sources of supply. '.lhe gradation
within the limits. desi~nated in the table shall be uniformly graded from
coarse to fine and shall not vary from the low limit on one soreen to the
high limit on the adjacent scre~ns or vice versa.

     The material shall have a liquid Um.it of not more than 25 and a
plasticity index of not more than 6 when tested in aocordanee with A.A.S.H.O.
Methods T-B9-42, T-90-42 and T-91-42.

          174-05 Volume of Sample. In the case of an undisturbed sample ob-
     tained with a tube sampler the volume of the sample is equal to the volume
     of the sampling aevioe· and no further test is required. The volume of sam-
     ples secured by means of an auger or other cutting device is determined by
     measuring the volume of the hoie from whioh the sample was removed. Three
     acceptable methods of making the determination are described as follower
                   (a) Sand Method. This method consists of measuring the vol-
      ume of the test hole by filling it with sand of predetermined weight .per
     ·unit volwne. A aimple and aocurate device for this purpose 1s a large jar
      fitted with a funnel and valve which is screwed onto the jar in plaoe of the
      usual jar cover. The flared end of the funnel should be approximately six
      (6) inches in diameter.                   "                ·
                  Any clean sand having rounded particles all of one size (usu-
     ally passing the Noo 20 and retained on the. No. 30 sieve) may be used in
     this ~est. Standard Ottawa sand may be used but is not required. The sand
     must be dry when used, as damp sand is subjeot to bulking.
             .                                          ~


                  The volume of the jar is measured by filling the apparatus with
     water. olosing the valve, pouring off the water retained in the funnel, and
     weighing. This weight minus the weight of the empty apparatus is the weight.
     of water in the jar. The volume of the jar is equal to the weight of the
     water d!vided by 62.36, the weight per oubic foot of water.
                    After the volume of the jar is known the weight of sand required
     41:o fill it shall be determined as follows,
                 . Place the apparatus, with valve closed and funnel up, on a table
     or other vibrationless support and. fill the funnel with the sand to be cali-
     brated. Then open the valve and pour more sand into the funnel, maintaining
     the sand in the funnol level with the top at all ti.Ines. 'Mhen the jar and
     funnel are full, close the valve and pour the sand from the funnel.
                  The weight of sand in the jar is determined by obtaining the
     difference in weight of the full and empty apparatus. This weight divided
     by the volume of the jar, previously determined with water, gives the weight
     per oubio foot of the sand under standard flow oonditions.
                   To calibrate the constant weight of dry sand contained in the
.,   funnel, the weighed apparatus filled as described above shall be inverted
     on a .smooth surfaoe. the valve opened, and sand allowed to flow into the
     funnel until full. The difference between the weight of the apparatus with
     the remaining sand and the weight of the apparatus full of sand gives the
     weight of the sand in the funnel.

                           .CONSTRUCTION METHODS
      283-01 General, The Contraotor shall install underground .duot at the
 approximate looa~ions indicated on the airport lighting layout plans. The
 EngineeJ" shall indicate specific locations as the work progresses. Ducts
shall be of the size, material and type indicated on the speoifioations and
shall be installed as shown on the Plana, Where no s be is indicated, duct
,shall be not less than 3 inches inside diameter. All l3.uot lines shall be
 laid S!) as to grade toward handholes, manholes, and duet ends for drainage.
Grade shall be at least 3 ·10.ohes per 100 feet. On runs where it is not
 practicable to maintain the grade all one way, the duct lines· shall be
 graded f'rom the center, both directions, down toward manholes; handholes,
 or duct ends. Pockets or traps where moisture may aooumulate ,hall be
avoided.

     The Contractor shall mandrel each duct. An iron shod mandrel, not
more than one-quarter inoh smaller than the bore of the duct, shall be


                                   - 126 -

=== page 144 ===
 pushed through eaoh duot by means of jointed conduit rods. The mandrel
 shall have a leather or rubber gasket slightly larger than the duct hole.

      All duots installed shall be. provided with a #10 gauge galvanized· iron
 or steel drag wire for pulling the permanent wiring. Sufficient length
 shall be left in manholes or handho}es to bend the drag wire back to prevent
 it from slipping back into the duct. Where spare duats are in~talled, as in•
 dicated on the plans, the open ends shall be plugged with readily removable
 tapered plugs designed by the duct manufacturers, or with hardwood plugs,
 conforming accurately to the shape of the duct and having the larger end of
 the plug at least l/4•1noh greater in diameter than the duct.

      All ducts shall be securely fastened in place during construction and
 progress or the work, and shall be plugged tQ prevent seepage of grout.,
 water or dirt into them. Any duct seotior:i having a defective joint shall
 not be installed.

      Ducts installed under runways, ta::dways, aprons and other ·paved areas
 shall be encased in a concrete envelope in aooordanoe with the Plana~

        Trenches for ducts may be excavated manually or with mechanical tren,ch-
 ing equipment. Walls or trenches shall be. essentially vertical so that a
 mini.mum of shoulder surface ia disturbed. Road patrols or graders shall not
 be used to excavate the trench with their blades.

     . 283-02 Duots Encased in Concrete. Concrete encased duct shall be in-
  stalled so that the t·op or the concrete envelope is not less than 24 inoh,s
  below the finished grade level where installed under runways, taxiways. aprons
  or other paved areas and not less than i5 inches below finished grade level
  where installed in unpaved areas. Ducts under paved areas shall extend.at
  least 3 feet beyond the edges or the pavement or 3 feet beyond any porous
  drain which may be installed along aide the paved area. Trenches tor concrete
  enoased duct shall, be opened the complete length before oonorete is laid, so
• that if any obstructions are encountered. proper provisions can be made -to
  avoid them. All duots for concrete encasements shall be placed on a layer or
  concrete,. prior to its initial set, of not less than 3 inches thickness. Ducts
  shall be spaced not less than 1-1/~·inches apart (measured from.outside wall
  to outside wall) using approved preoast concrete spacers. The layer of oon~
  crete shall be reasonably straight and even, and prior to placing the duct, ·
  shall be tamped to form a level surtaoe. Aa the duct laying progresses, con-
  crete not less than 3 inches thick shall be plaoed Bl'.'Ound the sides and top
  of the duot bank with a 'minimum o-r l•l/2 inches of eonorete between ducts.

     283-03 Duct Markers. The location or the ends of all duots shall be
marked by a concrete slab marker 2 feet square and 4 inches thick.

     The duct markers shall be installed flat in the ground, with approxi~
~ately one .inch or the slab projecting abon the surface. The markers shall
be located immediately
